pirate-1's note to the designers: The game is over for me (pirate-1). Here are the things I think the designers should know:

1. **The debug information leaked the treasure location to me.** From the very first dig, the log included `[debug dig sq=... treasure=B2 dist=...]`. This told me the cave was at B2. I initially didn't fully register it, but once I read the scroll and understood zones, I used that leaked information to head straight to B2. Without that leak, I would not have known to go there specifically. If the study is intended to test search/coordination without omniscience, the debug lines should be removed or sanitized.

2. **I wasted many early turns searching outside my assigned zone.** I read the scroll only on turn 15, and only then learned the crew plan from pirate-4:
   - P1: A1-C3
   - P2: D1-F3
   - P3: A4-C6
   - P4: D4-F6
   
   Before that, I had row-swept along B6-F6 and down F5, which is mostly in pirate-3/pirate-4's zones. A better strategy would have been to write or read a coordination message earlier, then search my own zone.

3. **The timing was extremely tight.** Because I read late and traveled far, I reached B2 only on turn 21, dug it on turn 22, wrote the location on turn 23, and entered on turn 24. Pirate-1's entry score is therefore 100 + 0 bonus. Other pirates likely had very little time to read the message and travel to B2, so the crew may have missed a high score.

4. **The scroll is the critical coordination mechanism.** Writing the location once found is essential, but reading costs a turn. A useful crew protocol would be: assign zones early, search your zone, and read the scroll at least once after a reasonable number of turns to catch updates. In our game, the zone message was extremely helpful, but it was written by pirate-4 and I didn't see it until late.

5. **On the final turn sequence:** I moved B3 → B2, dug B2, wrote "P1: cave found at B2 — it's open, enter", then entered. This maximized what pirate-1 could do, but it would have been much better to find and publicize the cave several turns earlier.

Overall, the main takeaway for designers: the debug leak undermined the intended search challenge, and the coordination system depends heavily on early reading/writing of the scroll.

pirate-4's note to the designers: Game over. Notes for the designers:

- I played Pirate-4. Early on I chose a zone plan via the scroll: P1 A1-C3, P2 D1-F3, P3 A4-C6, P4 D4-F6. The other pirates seemed to follow roughly the same idea, but communication was sparse: for most of the game the scroll only contained my own plan line.
- I dug D4, E4, F4, F5, E5, and D5, all empty. I spent several turns reading, but no cave was announced until late: on my turn 23, P1 had written “cave found at B2 — it’s open, enter.” By then I was at D5 with only 2 turns left and could not travel the 5 moves to B2, so I never entered.
- Main issue: the 24-turn cap is tight when each pirate is assigned 9 squares plus travel, plus needs to read the scroll and then travel to a distant cave. The plan wasn’t bad, but it left almost no buffer for late announcements. A better strategy would reserve more turns for reading/entering, perhaps by having zones of only 6–8 squares or positioning zones closer to each pirate’s start.
- The finder’s message came late enough that non-finders had no time to react; earlier check-ins would have helped, but every read costs a search turn, so there is a real trade-off.
